## 1. System Overview & Decisions

### 1.1 Document Metadata & Purpose

NEO CRM is a faithful, self-hosted clone of the reference CRM workspace at
`https://neo-crm-8ab2c17c.base44.app/` (recon artifacts in `docs/`), rebuilt
on an open, single-process stack. Use this PAD to understand how the system is
wired, why each choice was made, and where the sharp edges are. A new engineer
should read sections 1–3 then skim 9; anyone debugging auth, the SQLite path
resolution, or the mobile navigation should read sections 3.3 and 6 in full.

The clone's one historical behavioral divergence — **mobile navigation** —
was upstreamed: the reference's 2026-10 parity event (probe #119) fixed its
own 100+-session defect (its sidebar hid below `md` with no replacement).
NEO CRM now mirrors that fix (the Radix Sheet construction, walk37-decoded)
and keeps its behavioral supersets: the resize-past-md auto-close, the
main-scroller lock, the route-change close — all pinned by the dedicated
E2E regression suite.

### 1.2 Technology Stack Summary

| Layer | Technology | Version | Key Rationale |
| ----- | ---------- | ------- | ------------- |
| Web framework | Next.js (App Router) | 16.4.2 | Single-process RSC + API routes; async `cookies()`/`params`; standalone output for self-contained deploys |
| UI runtime | React | 19.3 | Async params, `inert` as a boolean prop, no `forwardRef`; lint-enforced hook discipline |
| Language | TypeScript | 5.9 | Full strict mode (session-174: the `noImplicitAny: false` escape hatch retired — the digma graft per the repo's own evaluation report §9.2); the build is the type gate (`ignoreBuildErrors` retired) with the explicit `typecheck` step as the faster signal |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS | 4.3.3 | CSS-first `@theme` tokens; zero JS config; validated against `docs/Tailwind-V4-Validation-Report.md` |
| Animation | tw-animate-css (vendored) | 1.4.0 | npm package exposes only the `style` export condition — unsupported by Turbopack, so the dist CSS is vendored |
| UI primitives | Radix UI | per-package | Dialog, Popover, Select, Label, Slot already in scaffold; hand-rolled tabs/tables/checkboxes with native ARIA |
| Charts | recharts | 3.10.1 | Session-176 (the N-3 retirement): the 2.15 → 3.x major; render-identical on the standing drift gates, `accessibilityLayer={false}` pinned at every site for the reference's 2.x wrapper DOM parity |
| Icons | lucide-react | 0.525 | Sole icon set; two-class stroke system (nav 1.8 / content 2.0) |
| ORM | Prisma | 6.19.3 | Schema-first single-file model; `db push` keeps zero-migration dev loop |
| Database | SQLite | (libsqlite) | Zero-config local story; path resolution normalized in `src/lib/db-path.ts` |
| State | Zustand | 5.0.15 | One client store for all server state — no React Query/SWR layer to keep in sync |
| Unit tests | Vitest | 5.0 | Fast node-environment seam tests; `@` alias resolution |
| E2E tests | Playwright | 1.64 | Real Chromium against the standalone production build on :3100 |

### 1.3 Architecture Decision Records (ADRs)

**ADR-001: Next.js 16 App Router single app (no monorepo)**

- **Context:** The reference is a single-page platform app; the operator
  wants a self-contained clone that runs with one command and pushes to one
  repo. The sibling scandihaven project demonstrates a pnpm/Turborepo
  monorepo, but its multi-package overhead buys nothing at this scope.
- **Decision:** One Next.js application; `src/app` route groups partition
  public (`/login`, `/signup`) from authenticated (`(app)`) surfaces; API
  handlers live under `src/app/api`.
- **Rationale:** Single deploy artifact, one lockfile, one test runner;
  the `(app)` layout guard replaces a middleware/proxy entirely.
- **Consequences:** + Simple mental model, trivial local setup. − No
  independent scaling of API vs UI (acceptable: SQLite is single-node anyway).
- **Alternatives Rejected:** Turborepo monorepo (scandihaven pattern) —
  premature at this scale; separate Express API — loses RSC and doubles
  surface area.

**ADR-002: Prisma + SQLite with normalized relative-path resolution**

- **Context:** The Prisma CLI resolves relative `file:` URLs against
  `prisma/schema.prisma`, but the runtime resolves them against the process
  CWD — and the standalone server chdirs into `.next/standalone`. Without
  normalization, dev, CLI and production land on three different database
  files (this failure was observed live during E2E bring-up). A second
  hazard surfaced in the session-2 audit: **bun loads `.env` for every
  `bun run`/`bun x` process and rewrites a relative `file:` DATABASE_URL
  into an absolute path resolved against the `.env` file's own directory** —
  with the root contract `file:../db/custom.db` that is one directory
  OUTSIDE the repo, and absolute URLs pass through the resolver untouched
  (observed live: the dev server opened `<parent-of-repo>/db/custom.db`).
- **Decision:** `src/lib/db-path.ts` re-implements the CLI rule at runtime
  (anchor order: standalone-CWD detector → module repo-root validator →
  plain CWD with an existence guard). Validated anchors `mkdir -p` the
  `db/` folder (first boot). `runtimeDatabaseUrl()` additionally parses the
  `.env` at the process CWD and, when the env var is exactly bun's
  absolutization of the `.env` value, re-derives the URL from the RAW value
  via the schema rule; any other env value (e2e override, postgres, an
  intentional absolute path) wins untouched. `src/lib/db.ts`,
  `prisma/seed.ts` and the `scripts/prisma-env.ts` wrapper (`db:push`) all
  derive their URL through this seam, so every consumer lands on
  `<repo>/db/<name>`.
- **Rationale:** Keeps `DATABASE_URL=file:../db/custom.db` working
  identically across `next dev`, `next build`, `next start`, Prisma CLI and
  the Playwright webServer — pinned by `tests/db-path.test.ts` (20 checks:
  urlForRoot first-boot mkdir, parseEnvFile, effectiveDatabaseUrl
  re-anchoring, runtimeDatabaseUrl discovery, plus the original passthrough
  and anchoring contract).
- **Consequences:** + Zero-config dev, no migration ceremony. − SQLite is
  single-writer; swapping to PostgreSQL later means changing the datasource
  block and re-verifying the path seam.
- **Alternatives Rejected:** Drizzle + Postgres 17 (scandihaven stack) —
  requires a running database service; absolute production paths in `.env` —
  cwd-fragile in containers; `prisma/.env` split — bun keeps loading the
  root `.env` first, so the split does not remove the hazard (verified
  empirically); `bun --env-file` in scripts — the flag does not chain into
  `bun x prisma` invocations.

**ADR-003: Hand-rolled scrypt + HMAC cookie sessions (no auth library)**

- **Context:** Two authenticated surfaces exist (login page + 29 route
  handler files). NextAuth/Better-Auth pull provider abstractions, adapters
  and their own DB schema for what is, here, a single email/password flow.
  The scaffold family already standardizes a minimal hand-rolled pattern.
- **Decision:** `src/lib/auth.ts` + the pure `src/lib/password.ts` seam:
  scrypt password hashes (`scrypt:salt:hash`), HMAC-SHA256-signed stateless
  cookie `neo_session` (7-day TTL) carrying the **tokenVersion revocation
  dimension** (`uid,iat,exp,v` — session-163, the digma S67-A port: a
  password reset increments the live row's version and every pre-reset
  cookie dies at the `getSessionUser` comparison; a missing `v` reads as 0),
  `verifySessionToken` with `timingSafeEqual`, the login timing equalizer
  (the miss branch burns the same scrypt), `requireSession()` guard used by
  every handler, auth routes rate-limited per IP/15min (login 10, signup 10,
  resend 5, verify 20, forgot 10, reset 10) keyed on the LAST x-forwarded-for
  hop behind the deploy-declared `NEO_PROXY_HOPS` depth. The password-reset
  funnel (session-163): forgot-password/reset-password routes (atomic
  single-use tokens + the eviction) + the `/reset-password` landing page.
  The auth family validates through the Zod seam (`src/lib/schemas.ts` +
  `parseBody()` — session-163).
- **Rationale:** ~184 auditable lines, zero extra dependencies, no external
  identity provider needed for a self-hosted clone; the reference's Google
  button is rendered for parity but degrades to an explanatory toast (no
  OAuth credentials exist in a self-hosted clone — documented deviation, not
  a silent failure).
- **Consequences:** + No library churn, full control of cookie flags.
  − Rotating `AUTH_SECRET` invalidates all sessions (acceptable, documented);
  no SSO until deliberately added.
- **Alternatives Rejected:** NextAuth v4 (in scaffold's allowed stack) —
  adapter + provider ceremony outweighs a two-field login; Better-Auth —
  same, plus its own migration story.

**ADR-004: One Zustand store for all server state + `{ok,data}` envelope**

- **Context:** Nine entity pages all need the same slices (accounts,
  contacts, leads, activities, events, settings, dashboard, users), plus
  cross-cutting concerns (global search, quick-create from anywhere).
  Introducing React Query would add cache keys, retries and hydration rules
  for a dataset that fits in memory and is only mutated by one user at a
  time.
- **Decision:** `src/stores/crm-store.ts` — a single store with a `call()`
  fetch client that understands the envelope, `hydrate()` bootstrapping every
  slice after `/api/auth/me`, and per-entity CRUD actions that refresh the
  affected slices. All 41 API handlers respond with `ok()`/`fail()` from
  `src/lib/api.ts` (session-68 F-68b1: the stale "22" carrier refreshed —
  29 route files / 41 verb handlers, the count the audit censuses carry).
- **Rationale:** One place to reason about client cache; mutations always
  leave the store internally consistent (action → API → refetch slice);
  envelope gives uniform error UX via toasts.
- **Consequences:** + No query-key sprawl; trivially greppable data flow.
  − Refetch granularity is per-slice, not per-item (fine at demo scale).
- **Alternatives Rejected:** TanStack Query — cache complexity without a
  multi-user freshness requirement; React Context — re-render storms.

**ADR-005: Tailwind v4 CSS-first with literal-hex tokens and vendored animation CSS**

- **Context:** Tailwind v4 removes the JS config; its documented pitfalls
  (`@theme` `var()` chains silently dropped; `hidden` attribute overriding
  display utilities; plugin/PostCSS misconfiguration producing "flat look")
  were validated in `docs/Tailwind-V4-Validation-Report.md`. The scaffold
  shipped **without `postcss.config.mjs`**, which silently disabled the
  entire pipeline (observed: fully unstyled pages with parse warnings at the
  `@theme` directive). The `tw-animate-css` npm package additionally exposes
  only the `style` export condition, which Turbopack's CSS resolver rejects.
- **Decision:** All design tokens are literal hex in one `@theme` block
  (`src/app/globals.css`); custom utilities via `@utility`; the PostCSS
  config pins `@tailwindcss/postcss`; `tw-animate-css` is vendored at
  `src/app/vendor/tw-animate.css` and imported relatively.
- **Rationale:** Each rule prevents an observed failure: missing PostCSS
  config → unstyled app; package import → `Module not found: Can't resolve
  'tw-animate-css'`; `var()` chains → silently dropped tokens.
- **Consequences:** + Reproducible styling pipeline. − Animation library
  updates require re-vendoring (tracked in Known Issues).
- **Alternatives Rejected:** `@config` bridge to a JS config — legacy path,
  loses v4 features; `tailwindcss-animate` JS plugin — not loadable
  CSS-first.

**ADR-006: Mobile navigation drawer (deliberate fix over the reference)**

- **Context:** Verified on the live reference at 390×844: below `md` the
  sidebar vanishes and the only interactive chrome is the avatar dropdown
  (Profile/Logout). No hamburger, no drawer — the app is unusable on phones.
- **Decision:** `src/components/layout/mobile-nav.tsx` — always-mounted
  drawer (`inert` + `visibility:hidden` when closed so it is removed from
  tab order and from Playwright/AT visibility), CSS-transform slide
  animation, focus trap with Tab cycling, Escape close with focus restore,
  dual scroll-lock with original-value cleanup (`document.body` AND the
  `main` scroller — since session 6 `main` is the app's scroll container),
  close-on-route-change via
  adjust-during-render (React 19 lint-safe), and auto-close when the viewport
  grows past `md` (session-7: the drawer and trigger are `md:hidden`,
  matching the reference sidebar's `hidden md:flex`). Pinned by
  `tests/e2e/mobile-navigation.spec.ts` (11 checks, 390/700px viewports).
- **Rationale:** Restores the primary navigation affordance the reference
  lost; every behavior maps to a documented Tailwind-v4/React-19 failure
  class in the skills research (overlay clipping, z-index wars, scroll-lock
  leaks, setState-in-effect).
- **Consequences:** + Phone users get full navigation; regression suite
  prevents silent reintroduction. − One more layout component to keep in
  sync with the sidebar (shared `SidebarNav` keeps them in lockstep).
- **Alternatives Rejected:** Bottom tab bar — diverges visually from the
  reference sidebar; no fix (reference behavior) — unacceptable.

**ADR-007: React-19-lint-clean dialog patterns (remount-via-key)**

- **Context:** The scaffold's ESLint enforces `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`
  as an error. The classic "reset form state when the dialog opens" effect
  violates it; naive fixes (refs, flags) just move the smell.
- **Decision:** Every entity dialog (`src/components/shared/entity-dialogs.tsx`)
  is split into a shell + form: the shell mounts the form only while open,
  keyed by entity id; the form initializes **all** state via `useState`
  initializers at mount. No dialog component contains a single effect-based
  setState.
- **Rationale:** The pattern is the React-docs-sanctioned way to "reset state
  with a key"; it is also bug-free against double-open / switch-edit-target
  races, and keeps the store's settings snapshot as the single source for
  defaults.
- **Consequences:** + Zero lint suppressions; predictable form lifecycles.
  − Slightly more files/props than an effect-based reset.
- **Alternatives Rejected:** Guarded setState-in-render inside one component
  — legal but fragile under concurrent rendering; disabling the rule —
  erodes the repo-wide guarantee.

---

**ADR-014: In-app OTP/reset deliveries behind production kill-switches**

- **Context:** A self-hosted demo/first-run posture needs working signup
  verification and password reset WITHOUT a wired mail transport — the
  s163 auth retrofit (the digma port) had both families answering their
  codes/links in the API response (ADR-014's in-app deliveries), while a
  production deployment with a real mail transport must NOT leak them.
- **Decision:** Both deliveries sit behind env knobs:
  `NEO_DISABLE_IN_APP_OTP="1"` stops the signup/resend responses
  carrying `verificationCode`; `NEO_DISABLE_IN_APP_RESET="1"` stops the
  forgot-password response carrying `resetUrl` (the s163 e2e family
  rides the unset defaults). The no-enumeration 200 is unchanged either
  way; a deployment wires its mail transport and knobs both OFF.
- **Rationale:** The demo UX stays honest (the verify view's code hint,
  the sent card's reset link — all rendered from the response), while
  the production channel closes with one env change — no code path
  forks, no dual response shapes to test.
- **Consequences:** + One response shape per family, knobbed at the
  edge. − The env contract grows two knobs (§3.3's table carries them);
  a knobbed-off deployment needs the transport itself (out of repo
  scope, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`).
- **Alternatives Rejected:** Always answering the codes (a silent
  credential leak in production); never answering them (the demo
  posture loses its working flows — the pre-s163 client-side theater
  defect class).

**ADR-015: The resilience family — the error boundaries, the boot gate, the smoke leg, the artifact hygiene (session-170)**

- **Context:** The s169 round closed the runtime secret gate (the
  first-session-op throw) but three §6.2/§13.4/§14 checklist legs were
  still open: no error boundaries anywhere (an uncaught render error
  shipped Next's stock crash page — no brand, no recovery, no digest),
  no boot-time env validation (a misconfigured production boot failed
  late or never signaled), and no smoke leg between build and e2e (the
  web-app-foundation gate order's missing step). AND the audit's
  critical discovery: the standalone artifact traced the WHOLE repo root
  (the upload family's runtime-computed fs paths defeat @vercel/nft's
  static analysis) — shipping `.env` (the real AUTH_SECRET), `.git/`
  (171 MB), `db/` (the dev SQLite with user rows), `uploads/`, `skills/`
  (194 MB) and `docs/` (49 MB) in every deployable (~550 MB where ~151
  MB suffices); Next's own `writeStandaloneDirectory` additionally
  copies `.env` explicitly (immune to tracing excludes).
- **Decision:** Four surfaces, one rotation. (1) `src/app/error.tsx` +
  `src/app/global-error.tsx` — the scandihaven-ported boundary pair
  (the house-styled card, the digest ref, the `reset()` Try-again; the
  stale-chunk self-heal via `src/lib/chunk-recovery.ts` — one guarded
  reload per 10s cooldown, the flag written BEFORE the reload;
  global-error renders its own html/body + imports its own globals.css;
  ZERO component-tree imports beyond the recovery seam). (2)
  `src/instrumentation.ts` → `src/lib/boot-env.ts` — the boot gate:
  production AUTH_SECRET missing/short THROWS AT BOOT (the
  phase-production-build skip keeps `next build` secretless — verified
  live); the NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL localhost misconfig + the unknown
  NEO_* typo family + off-vocabulary kill-switch values + unparsable
  proxy-hops WARN; dev/test boot soft. (3) `scripts/smoke-test.sh` +
  the `gate`/`gate:full` wiring — the §13.4/§14 smoke leg: the
  standalone boot on :3210 with its own `db/smoke.db` + a dedicated
  throwaway secret, the 14-check ladder (health · 401s · the login
  round-trip · the Zod 400 · the valid create · the logout data:null
  face · the 11th-login 429 · the pages · the SEO family · THE
  BOOT-GATE PROBE · THE ARTIFACT-HYGIENE assertions). (4)
  `outputFileTracingExcludes` in `next.config.ts` + the build script's
  `rm -f .next/standalone/.env` — the artifact-hygiene double-cure.
- **Rationale:** The boundaries are the reference's OWN gap (it ships
  none) — the superset posture the mobile drawer already established;
  the boot gate front-runs the s169 runtime throw with the same message
  family; the smoke leg polices the artifact contract every rebuild
  (the hygiene assertions ride check 14); the excludes keep the
  artifact at the server's true runtime needs (verified: the
  photo-e2e pair GREEN against the clean artifact — `prisma/schema.prisma`
  stays traced, `db/`/`uploads/` are mkdir'd on demand).
- **Consequences:** + A secretless production boot refuses to serve
  (the actionable message at boot, not the first sign-in); + the
  artifact is ~151 MB of server-only content; + the smoke leg catches
  envelope/auth/limiter regressions between build and e2e. − The gate
  chain grows one step (~25 s); − the boundaries are unreachable by
  honest e2e without a debug affordance (documented — the unit
  source-pins + the live captures carry that proof instead).
- **Alternatives Rejected:** Error boundaries importing the component
  tree (a resilience surface must not depend on the tree that might be
  broken); a `middleware.ts` boot check (middleware runs per-request,
  not at boot — the wrong seam); post-build artifact scrubbing outside
  the build script (invisible to `bun run build` consumers); leaving
  `.env` in the artifact (Next's designed behavior, but the documented
  production posture sets env at launch — the copy was never the
  mechanism, only an accident that shipped the dev secret).

---

**ADR-016: The a11y two-gate — the named-control family + the sanctioned register (session-171)**

- **Context:** The web-app-foundation §8.3 prescribes the "best-in-class
  add-on": an axe-core two-gate (structural violations must be zero;
  color-contrast nodes must cite the sanctioned palette). The s170 round
  named it as the next rotation's candidate. The live audit found three
  families: 34 unnamed controls on our open surfaces (19 SelectTrigger
  comboboxes + 11 inert Checkboxes + 4 phone-width icon-only Buttons —
  `role="combobox"` names come from author attributes only, so the
  visible span text is invisible to the accessible-name computation),
  the shared parity palette (color-contrast), and the recharts
  funnel-genus (`svg-img-alt` on library-hardcoded `role="img"` Layers).
  The reference ships all three identically — verified live on its own
  DOM.
- **Decision:** (1) Every open-surface interactive control carries an
  accessible name (aria-label — invisible supersets; the reference's
  nameless controls are its defect, ours name them; the drawer
  precedent). (2) `tests/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` rides the e2e harness as the
  two-gate: GATE 1 asserts structural violations zero (the named-control
  proof); GATE 2 asserts every color-contrast/svg-img-alt node cites the
  SANCTIONED register — the parity palette + the recharts genus, each
  matcher carrying its citation (fixing them would break visual parity
  or require patching library internals). (3) `axe-core` is a DECLARED
  devDep (^4.13.0) — the gate must not stand on a transitive standby.
- **Consequences:** New interactive controls must ship named (the
  a11y-controls census pins every open-surface SelectTrigger); a new
  contrast regression outside the sanctioned register REDs the e2e gate;
  the dialog-surface select family (closed by default, unprobed) is the
  documented next candidate; the register is matcher-scoped — a new
  unsanctioned family fails rather than slipping through.

**ADR-017: The closed-surface naming closure + the property-family doctrine (session-172)**

- **Context:** The s171 two-gate scans pages + the drawer-open state —
  but a Radix Dialog's content is ABSENT from the DOM while closed, so
  the dialog-surface controls were invisible to every probe AND to the
  gate (the closed-by-default genus). The live open-dialog audit (axe on
  each open state, ours + the reference's own open dialogs) found: 9
  nameless create-dialog SelectTriggers + the entity-edit-dialog family
  (5 nameless config-driven triggers + 17 unlabeled Inputs — the bare
  `<Label>{f.label}</Label>` forms no htmlFor/id association). The
  reference ships the identical nameless-combobox family on every
  dialog plus its own label violations — the fixes are invisible
  supersets (the s171 doctrine). The round also adopted the OTHER
  s170-named candidate: the fast-check property family (the round's
  one devDep bump; the no-mid-rotation-bump posture — s171 bumped
  axe-core, s172 bumped fast-check).
- **Decision:** (1) Every dialog-surface interactive control carries an
  accessible name: the create-dialog triggers take their sibling
  Label's exact text as `aria-label`; the edit-dialog wires the real
  association (`htmlFor`/`id` on the `edit-${f.key}` pair — the s115
  Defaults genus) + `aria-label={f.label}` on the config-driven
  combobox arm. (2) The two-gate EXTENDS to the open-dialog states —
  six explicit registrations (the five create dialogs + the contact
  edit dialog), the sanctioned register unchanged. (3) The pure seams
  carry PROPERTY tests (`tests/property.test.ts`, fast-check): every
  seam's round-trip/invariant/totality contract is pinned over a
  generated input space — a new seam violation family REDs the unit
  gate. (4) `parseCsv` normalizes line endings INSIDE the state
  machine, outside quotes only (RFC 4180 §2.6 — a quoted CRLF is field
  data; the pre-172 global replace mutated quoted cells silently).
- **Consequences:** New dialog controls must ship named + label-wired
  (the dialog-controls census pins the constructions); new pure seams
  ship with a property pin (the round-trip/totality doctrine); the
  csv round-trip property is the parser's regression guard; the
  property generators must respect the seam's OWN domain contracts
  (the three live-caught calibrations: the vocabulary intrusion · the
  undefined-typed field · the ragged-row normalization).

### ADR-018 — The injectable clock seam + the deeper property family (session-173)

- **Context:** The s172 close named the deeper property family as the
  next rotation: the schemas.ts Zod round-trips + the rate-limit window
  arithmetic — the latter blocked on a seam gap: `rateLimit()` read
  `Date.now()` internally, so no generator could drive the clock and the
  fixed-window semantics lived only under the example-based battery's
  wall-clock accidents (a 60s window cannot be waited out inside a
  test). The s164 sweep had ALREADY taken `now = Date.now()`
  (`sweepRateLimits` + the pure `computeWatermark` seam) — the
  rateLimit path had never joined its own module's injectable form.
- **Decision:** (1) `rateLimit(key, limit, windowMs, now = Date.now())`
  — the s164 form extended to the rateLimit path, PURELY ADDITIVE (the
  default is the wall clock; all 7 call sites ride unchanged; zero
  behavior change on the wire). (2) The property battery grows IN PLACE
  (`tests/property.test.ts`, the ADR-017 one-suite doctrine — SUITES
  held): the Zod round-trip half (P-h…P-m — the create-defaults
  quadrant · the enum membership · the emailField trim/lowercase
  contract · the PUT three-state · the required-name quadrant · the
  contacts legacy priority mapping) + the rate-limit window half
  (P-n…P-q — the fixed-window reset crossing · the retryAfterSec
  formula · the remaining monotonicity · the default-clock
  equivalence). (3) The clock-seam example battery joins the seam's own
  suite (`tests/rate-limit.test.ts` +3, incl. the additive-signature
  source pin).
- **Consequences:** A time-dependent seam ships with an injectable
  clock (the sweep precedent now the module-wide rule); the stateful
  property family appends a per-run monotonic run id to its keys (the
  L-173c3 lesson: the module-level bucket map persists across fc runs —
  uniqueness is a property of the RUN, not of the generated input); the
  N-67b shape pin carries the additive signature; NO new devDep
  (fast-check rides the s172 declaration).

### ADR-019 — The build-is-the-type-gate doctrine + full strictness + the single lockfile (session-174)

- **Context:** The repo's own newest artifacts (commit `25f1847` —
  `skills/web-application/SKILL.md` v1.0.0, the four-sibling family
  distillation, + `skills/web-application/codebase-evaluation-report.md`,
  the comparative audit ranking neo-crm #1 while naming its defect
  register) prescribe three hardening grafts (report §9.2): drop
  `ignoreBuildErrors` (the zero-balance graft), drop
  `noImplicitAny: false` (the digma graft), and one lockfile (Appendix A
  lesson 14). The code disagreed with the repo's own distilled doctrine
  — so the code is what got fixed (the code-wins rule in reverse).
- **Decision:** (1) `next.config.ts`'s `typescript: {
  ignoreBuildErrors: true }` block RETIRED — the build IS the type gate
  (a planted type error fails `bun run build`; the gate-proof ran the
  SAME plant both ways: swallowed under the old posture, caught under
  the new one). The explicit `typecheck` script stays in the gate chain
  as the FASTER signal (the §15 fast-gates-first order holds). (2)
  `tsconfig.json`'s `"noImplicitAny": false` RETIRED — full TS
  strictness; the flip surfaced exactly three stragglers repo-wide
  (`scripts/sweep.ts`'s inline union-find twin — now typed verbatim like
  its fully-typed pure seam). (3) `package-lock.json` RETIRED — one
  package manager (bun), one lockfile (`bun.lock`); the
  `outputFileTracingExcludes` entry stays as the defensive tripwire.
  The config source-pin battery `tests/type-gate.test.ts` (the 138th
  suite, +5) holds all three moves + the typed arms + the gate-chain
  order.
- **Consequences:** A type error can no longer compile into the
  deployable artifact (the skipped-command hazard closed — the build
  enforces what only the manual step used to catch); full strictness
  means every future implicit-any fails `tsc` AND the build (the three
  surfacing stragglers proved the src/ tree was already clean — the
  escape hatch protected nothing but the stragglers); the npm-world
  path (`install_packages.sh`) was verified lockfile-independent before
  the retirement (it installs by package NAME from the registry); the
  s61/s62 §16ba two-lockfile regen rule is superseded (its AGENTS/PAD
  narrative rows stay as history).

### ADR-020 — The response-side wire-contract doctrine: the READ half is a stated contract, not a rendering coincidence (session-175)

- **Context:** The request side had its contracts stated twice over —
  the Zod seam at every body boundary (s163/166) and the fast-check
  property family over the schemas (s173 P-h…P-m). The READ side — what
  `ok(rows)` ships to the client store — had NO battery of its own: the
  wire shapes (the routes' include select sets, the orderBy contracts,
  the envelope's passthrough discipline, the Date→ISO serialization the
  client's format family consumes, the null-preservation the `??`
  fallback chains depend on) were pinned only indirectly, through the
  e2e's RENDERED output. A wire-shape regression that keeps the UI
  rendering (e.g. a dropped joined field the current seed never
  exercises as null) would have landed silently.
- **Decision:** The READ side is now a stated contract in two layers.
  (1) The property family (`tests/property.test.ts` P-r/P-s): every one
  of the six entities' ROUTE-EXACT rows survives the wire serialization
  verbatim — scalars unchanged, every Date as its exact `toISOString()`
  form, nulls PRESENT, the key set exact, the joined shapes intact; the
  envelope pair carries its shape as a property (`ok()` two-key
  passthrough, `fail()` verbatim error arm — driven through the real
  helpers). (2) The source pins (`tests/crud-zod-seam.test.ts`, the
  ROUTE-side suite's other half): the five include-carrying routes'
  exact include select sets, the six list routes' orderBy contracts, the
  every-GET-through-the-envelope doctrine (s42), the opportunities
  raw-row contract (s31), and the me route's full SessionUser carrier.
- **Consequences:** A wire-shape change (an include select edited, an
  orderBy flipped, an envelope key added, a projection field dropped)
  now fails the unit gate at the seam level BEFORE any rendered symptom
  — mutation-proven both ways this round (the planted include regression
  and the planted envelope key each tripped exactly one pin). The
  contract is per-shape, not per-Prisma-semantics: the generators state
  the wire's field-level invariants independently of join-nullity
  coupling. The login response's slim 5-field user shape is documented
  as a zero-consumer divergence (the login card reads only `body.ok`);
  the canonical user carrier is `auth/me` — pinned with `photoUrl`.

### ADR-021 — The chart-major migration doctrine: parity props + version-borne interiors never enter the layout alignment (session-176)

- **Context:** The recharts 2.15 → 3.10.1 migration (the report's N-3
  retirement — the last named register item touching product code).
  The official 3.0 guide's breaking census against the repo's nine
  chart-type JSX sites surfaced exactly one DOM-visible default flip:
  `accessibilityLayer` ships false in 2.x, TRUE in 3.0+ — the chart
  wrappers would gain `role="region"`/`tabIndex`/`aria-label` the
  reference's 2.x DOM does not carry. The live migration also moved the
  tick-label texts into per-axis z-index layers, elided the zero-value
  bar rectangles (2.15 rendered degenerate zero-height wrappers), and
  restructured the chart interiors into `recharts-zIndex-layer_*`
  groups — which the interior census's ALIGNER chased into false resync
  offsets (the tablet-dashboard decode: a phantom +581px cross-card
  re-pair over apps a direct probe proved byte-equal at every card
  position).
- **Decision:** Two standing rules. (1) **The parity-prop discipline**:
  every chart-type site pins `accessibilityLayer={false}` explicitly —
  the reference's 2.x wrapper DOM is the parity surface; turning the
  3.x layer ON is an owner-level decision (it is our addition, not a
  reference mirror — the a11y two-gate's sanctioned register was
  calibrated on the 2.x genus), documented here as the enable path.
  (2) **The version-borne chart interior never enters the layout
  alignment**: the census collector records the recharts svg BOX (the
  chart's layout face, with the wrapper divs) and skips its subtree —
  the walk42 glyph-interior doctrine extended to the chart svg; the
  chart visual + DOM parity rides the pixel sweeps + the e2e chart pins
  (the L-138c2 grid sentinel — the standing "future recharts upgrade"
  guard, which passed live on 3.10.1 as this round's upgrade-proof).
  The decoder keeps a signature-matched `recharts3-restructure` genus
  for any chart-interior events that do reach it (both sides
  g-elements, one side carrying a 3.x structural token — never a
  `/recharts/` catch-all; the negatives pinned: a DIV pair and a
  same-class non-degenerate tick with a real drift stay RED).
- **Consequences:** A future chart-library major (recharts 4.0 — the
  `Cell` → `shape` refactor is already named there) migrates without
  re-deriving the pixel baselines when the rendered output is
  byte-stable (this round's ledger: the six drift gates CLEAN at the
  migrated tree — render-identical, the standing baselines held), and
  without the census's alignment losing sync (the interiors are
  excluded by construction). The type seam stays at the family
  boundary: the formatters keep `(v: number) => string` (the numeric
  aggregates) behind the `tooltipFormatter` adapter, and the Pie label
  callback keeps the open-record param (the runtime truth recharts'
  own types cannot express) behind a documented bridge.

### ADR-022 — The interior re-walk doctrine: a surface's parity is only proven by walking its OPEN/ACTIVE DOM, and the v4 space-y margin-BOTTOM semantics is a standing per-surface hazard (session-177)

- **Context:** The drawer interior re-walk — the candidate six
  consecutive closes named (s171–s176) and no round ever ran. Every
  standing leg verifies a DIFFERENT face of the drawer: the battery
  its behavior (locks, focus, ESC, resize), the a11y two-gate its axe
  violations, the captures its visuals, the source pins its contract
  strings, the interior census its CLOSED state (52 page-axis runs,
  zero drawer-open). The drawer-OPEN interior — the element ledger
  both apps actually render — was never compared. walk50 (the
  two-app element-ledger tool, `scripts/walk50-drawer-interior.ts`)
  found exactly one live defect hiding in that gap: the drawer
  header's space-y-2 under v4's margin-BOTTOM semantics (the h2's
  `:where()` mb-2 in flow, header 88px) vs the reference's v3-era
  margin-TOP on the sr-only `<p>` (out of flow, header 80px) — an 8px
  spine shift present since the s147 re-derivation, invisible to every
  standing leg because none walks the open drawer's interior.
- **Decision:** Two standing rules. (1) **A parity-critical surface's
  OPEN/ACTIVE interior earns a walk when its risk profile changes**
  (a construction change, a Tailwind/react major, or the
  six-close nomination pattern) — the walk's form is the two-app
  element ledger (tag · class · aria · text · geometry, DFS-spine
  aligned), not a pixel sweep alone: pixels showed nothing (the
  8px shift sat inside the drawer the page sweeps never open). (2)
  **The v4 space-y margin-BOTTOM semantics is a PER-SURFACE hazard,
  cured per-surface with the explicit zero-margin override** (the
  M-92c1 `mb-0` precedent — (0,1,0) beats `:where()`'s (0,0,0)),
  never by rewriting the reference's literal class strings: the
  header's `space-y-2` stays byte-parity (the class-string contract),
  the computed rhythm goes byte-equal (the layout contract). The
  s11/s14/M-92c1/walk50 lineage is now the documented family: wherever
  a space-y group's gap semantics differs between the v3-era
  reference and our v4 build, the fix rides the CHILD (mt-2 / mb-0),
  the group's literal rides the reference.
- **Consequences:** The drawer-open interior's parity is now
  walk-proven (the post-cure spine byte-equal at every element; the
  two-app captures 0.02% apart — the font floor), the walk tool is a
  standing script (re-runnable the day the reference ships a new
  bundle — it pairs with probe:ref as the interior face of the
  fresh-bundle re-derivation), and the drawer's own genus register is
  documented (the token/literal idiom, the dead shadcn variants, the
  lucide aria-hidden, the useId format, the kept supersets each
  re-verified invisible). The next re-walk candidate list resets to
  the surfaces whose OPEN interiors have gone longest unwalked —
  adjudicated per the same doctrine.

## 2. High-Level System Topology

```mermaid
flowchart TB
  subgraph Client["Client Layer"]
    Desktop["Desktop browser ≥768px<br/>in-flow sidebar + topbar (main scrolls)"]
    Mobile["Mobile browser <1024px<br/>hamburger → drawer"]
  end

  subgraph Edge["Edge / Proxy (production, optional)"]
    TLS["TLS-terminating proxy<br/>(Secure cookies require HTTPS)"]
  end

  subgraph App["Application Layer — one Next.js process (:3000)"]
    RSC["(app) RSC pages<br/>server-side session guard"]
    Public["/login · /signup"]
    API["/api/* route handlers ×29<br/>force-dynamic · requireSession"]
    Shell["AppShell client island<br/>Zustand hydrate()"]
  end

  subgraph Data["Data Layer"]
    DB[("SQLite db/custom.db<br/>Prisma singleton · db-path seam")]
  end

  Desktop --> RSC
  Mobile --> RSC
  TLS --> App
  RSC --> DB
  API --> DB
  Shell -->|fetch envelope| API
  Public --> API
```

Runtime characteristics: a single Node/Bun process serves RSC pages, route
handlers and static chunks. Scaling is vertical only (SQLite is
single-writer); horizontal scaling requires the PostgreSQL swap documented in
ADR-002 consequences. There are no background workers, queues or external
service dependencies — every external integration point (email, OAuth) is a
documented graceful degradation.

---

## 3. Application Architecture

### 3.1 The Layer Model

```
Layer 0: Browser — the only "use client" leaves (AppShell, pages, dialogs,
         charts, store). Rule: client code never imports Prisma or lib/db.
Layer 1: RSC pages + (app) layout — resolve the session server-side,
         redirect unauthenticated visits, render client islands with props.
         Rule: no data fetching happens here beyond the session; pages own
         their data through the store.
Layer 2: API route handlers — 29 files / 41 handlers (incl. `PATCH
         /api/users` for the profile Full-Name edit), all `force-dynamic`,
         19 gated via `requireSession()` (the 10 ungated: the public
         auth family's 7 verbs [rate-limited instead] + `me` [the
         by-design logged-out data:null face, session-41] + `health`
         + the regex-pinned `uploads/[name]` serving — search IS
         gated). Rule: validation rides the Zod
         seam at the body boundary (`parseBody` + `src/lib/schemas.ts`);
         return the envelope, never throw across it.
Layer 3: Pure seams (src/lib) — auth, api envelope, db + db-path, format,
         csv, constants, rate-limit, download. Rule: unit-tested; handlers
         and pages import these instead of inlining logic.
Layer 4: Persistence — Prisma singleton over SQLite. Rule: import `db` from
         "@/lib/db" only; never construct PrismaClient directly.
```

**Golden Rule:** dependencies point strictly downward (Browser → RSC → API →
seams → Persistence). The store is the sole Browser-side data gateway; the
envelope is the sole wire format.

```
neo-crm/

│   └── seed.ts                  # idempotent demo workspace (in-place wipe + insert)
```

### 3.3 Critical Code Patterns

**Pattern 1 — The session guard (every handler, every page shell)**

```ts
// src/lib/api.ts — guard returns either the user or a ready-to-return 401.
export async function requireSession(): Promise<{ user: SessionUser } | { response: NextResponse }> {
  const user = await getSessionUser();
  if (!user) return { response: ERR.UNAUTHORIZED() };
  return { user };
}

// Typical handler usage — the isGuarded() narrowing keeps it two lines:
export async function GET() {
  const guard = await requireSession();
  if (isGuarded(guard)) return guard.response;
  const rows = await db.account.findMany({ /* … */ });
  return ok(rows);
}
```

*Why this pattern:* avoids the double-`await getSessionUser()` that a
`getSessionUser() ?? throw` API would force, keeps TypeScript narrowing
explicit, and makes the guard visible as the first statement of every
handler — greppable in security review.

**Pattern 2 — SQLite path normalization (the seam that keeps dev/prod/e2e identical)**

```ts
// src/lib/db-path.ts — mirror the Prisma CLI rule: relative file: URLs
// resolve against prisma/schema.prisma, not process.cwd().
const anchors: Array<() => string | null> = [
  repoRootFromStandaloneCwd, // cwd contains ".next" (standalone chdir) → repo is two levels up
  repoRootFromModule,        // this file's repo root, validated by schema.prisma on disk
  () => process.cwd(),       // last resort
];
for (const anchor of anchors) {
  const root = anchor();
  if (!root) continue;
  const candidate = join(root, "prisma", ref); // same shape the CLI computes
  if (existsSync(candidate) || existsSync(dirname(candidate))) return `file:${candidate}`;
}
```

*Why this pattern:* without it, `next dev`, `next build`, the standalone
server and the Playwright webServer each resolve `file:../db/custom.db`
against a different CWD and silently create/read different database files —
observed live as "login works but the dashboard redirects to /login"
(API chunk and page chunk held different stale handles).

**Pattern 3 — Remount-via-key dialogs (React-19-lint-clean forms)**

```tsx
// src/components/shared/entity-dialogs.tsx — shell mounts the form only
// while open, keyed by entity id; the form owns NO effects that setState.
export function AccountDialog({ open, onOpenChange, account }: Props) {
  return (
    <Dialog open={open} onOpenChange={onOpenChange}>
      <DialogContent className="max-w-xl">
        <DialogHeader>…</DialogHeader>
        {open && (
          <AccountForm key={account?.id ?? "new"} account={account ?? null} … />
        )}
      </DialogContent>
    </Dialog>
  );
}

function AccountForm({ account }: { account: Account | null; … }) {
  const { settings, users } = useCrmStore();
  const [form, setForm] = React.useState(() => ({
    tier: account?.tier ?? settings?.defaultTier ?? "B", // settings snapshot at mount
    /* … */
  }));
  // …
}
```

*Why this pattern:* `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` is an ERROR in this
repo. Keyed remount gives every open a pristine form initialized from
props/store — no reset effects, no stale-field bugs when switching the edit
target, and the store's settings snapshot is read exactly once.

**Pattern 4 — Mobile drawer lifecycle (the reference-app fix)**

```tsx
// src/components/layout/mobile-nav.tsx — close on route change WITHOUT
// setState-in-useEffect: adjust state during render (React 19 sanctioned).
const [prevPathname, setPrevPathname] = React.useState(pathname);
if (prevPathname !== pathname) {
  setPrevPathname(pathname);
  if (open) onOpenChange(false);
}
// …
// Closed state must be a real "hidden": inert removes it from the a11y tree
// and tab order; visibility:hidden (transitioned) keeps the slide-out
// animation while making `toBeHidden()` semantics honest for AT + tests.
<div
  className={cn("fixed inset-0 z-50 transition-[visibility] duration-300 lg:hidden",
    open ? "visible" : "invisible pointer-events-none")}
  role="dialog" aria-modal="true" inert={!open}
>
```

*Why this pattern:* every line answers a documented failure class —
Tailwind v4's `hidden`-attribute trap, scroll-lock leaks without cleanup,
focus left behind in a removed subtree, and drawers that stop closing when
JS state desyncs from the URL.

## 4. Data Architecture

### 4.1 Database Schema

```mermaid
erDiagram
  User ||--o{ Account : owns
  User ||--o{ Contact : owns
  User ||--o{ Lead : owns
  User ||--o{ Event : owns
  User ||--o{ Activity : owns
  Account ||--o{ Contact : houses
  Account ||--o{ Lead : relates
  Account ||--o{ Event : relates
  Account ||--o{ Activity : relates
  Contact ||--o{ Lead : references
  Contact ||--o{ Event : references
  Contact ||--o{ Activity : references

  User { string id PK  string email UK  string passwordHash
         string avatarColor  string role  datetime createdAt  datetime updatedAt }
  Account { string id PK  string name  string industry  float annualRevenue
            string tier  boolean isKey  string status  string ownerId FK
            datetime lastActivityAt }
  Contact { string id PK  string name  string email  string priority
            string source  string accountId FK  string ownerId FK
            datetime lastActivityAt }
  Lead { string id PK  string name  float value  string stage  string status
         string source  datetime expectedCloseDate  datetime closedAt
         datetime nextFollowUp  string accountId FK  string contactId FK }
  Event { string id PK  string title  string type  string status
          datetime startAt  datetime endAt  boolean allDay  string location }
  Activity { string id PK  string type  string subject  string status
             string priority  datetime dueAt  datetime completedAt }
  Setting { string id PK  string contactSources  string leadStages
            string activityTypes  string accountTiers  string industries
            string defaultCurrency  int followUpDays }
  SavedReport { string id PK  string name  string tab  string config }
```

Field notes: picklists (sources, stages, industries…) are stored as JSON
strings on the `Setting` singleton — settings round-trip as arrays through
`/api/settings`, parsed defensively with fallbacks. `Lead.status` is derived
from `stage` (`won`→`closed_won`, `lost`→`closed_lost`, else `open`) and
written transactionally by the API on every stage change. All relation
deletes are `SetNull` except `Activity→Account` and `Event→Account`
(`Cascade`) — deleting an account keeps its people but removes its logged
activities/events.

### 4.2 Data Models

Wire types live in `src/types/index.ts` (User, Account, Contact, Lead,
CrmEvent, Activity, Settings, Opportunity, plus the computed aggregates
`DashboardData` and `ReportsData` with their KPI shapes — the
`SavedReport` interface was retired at session-59; the localStorage
schema lives in `src/lib/saved-reports.ts`). Dates serialize as
ISO strings; `null` is used (never `undefined`) on the wire for optional
fields so JSON round-trips are lossless.

### 4.3 Persistence Strategy

- **Client instantiation:** one `PrismaClient` per process via
  `globalThis.__neoCrmPrisma` (`src/lib/db.ts`) — hot reload safe.
- **Schema evolution:** `bun run db:push` (no migrations directory). The seed
  (`prisma/seed.ts`) is idempotent: it wipes domain tables and reinserts —
  **in place**, never by deleting the file (a live server keeps reading the
  deleted inode; this exact failure was diagnosed and fixed during E2E
  bring-up).
- **Indexes:** every foreign key and every filtered column (`stage`,
  `status`, `dueAt`, `startAt`, `name`, `email`) is indexed.
- **Derived aggregates:** dashboard/report computations run server-side in
  their route handlers (single pass over leads/activities/users) — no
  SQL aggregates to port if the datasource changes.

---

## 5. Design System Reference

### 5.1 Typographic System

| Role | Face | Notes |
| ---- | ---- | ----- |
| Everything | **The system stack** (session-22: `ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif, "Apple Color Emoji", "Segoe UI Emoji", "Segoe UI Symbol", "Noto Color Emoji"` — pinned in `@theme --font-sans`) | The reference ships ZERO webfonts (no `@font-face`, `document.fonts` empty); the scaffold's Inter webfont was retired after measuring ~6-14% narrower text than the reference on the same string. No `antialiased`, no `text-rendering` override, no `::selection` tint (all browser defaults, like the reference) |
| KPI values | System 600, 28px, tight tracking | `text-[28px] font-semibold tracking-tight` |
| Card titles | System 600, 16px | `CardTitle` (the s13 re-pin — the base is 16px) |
| Table headers | System 500, muted ink, Title Case | `font-medium text-muted-ink` on `TableHead` (the session-3 parity fix — NOT uppercase/tracked; th inherits the 16px base) |
| Body/base | System 400, 16px | set on `body` in `globals.css` (the s13 pin) |

### 5.2 Color Tokens

Defined as **literal hex** in the single `@theme` block
(`src/app/globals.css`) — `var()` chains inside `@theme` are silently
dropped by the v4 build (validated in
`docs/Tailwind-V4-Validation-Report.md`).

| Token | Hex | Usage |
| ----- | --- | ----- |
| `--color-sidebar` | `#2563eb` | Sidebar/drawer background (measured from reference) |
| `--color-primary` | `#2563eb` | Primary buttons, active states, focus rings (the session-7 re-pin) |
| `--color-primary-hover` | `#1d4ed8` | Primary hover arms |
| `--color-background` | `#f9fafb` | App canvas |
| `--color-surface` | `#ffffff` | Cards, topbar, dialogs |
| `--color-foreground` | `#0a0a0a` | Primary text (the session-13 re-pin) |
| `--color-muted` | `#6b7280` | Secondary text |
| `--color-muted-ink` | `#737373` | Placeholders, tertiary text (the session-10 pin — NOT `--color-subtle`) |
| `--color-subtle` | `#9ca3af` | Tertiary text accents |
| `--color-line` | `#e5e5e5` | Borders, dividers (the session-12 split) |
| `--color-line-strong` | `#e5e7eb` | The stronger border family (since session-12) |
| `--color-line-soft` | `#f5f5f5` | Hover fills, input fills (the session-14 re-pin) |
| `--color-success` | `#10b981` | Won stage, positive deltas |
| `--color-warning` | `#f59e0b` | Proposal stage, key-account badges |
| `--color-danger` | `#ef4444` | Destructive actions, lost stage, overdue |
| Chart palette (stage/type hex) | `#3b82f6 #06b6d4 #eab308 #f97316 #9ca3af …` | Meta colors in `constants.ts` (session-4 DOM-pinned: Proposal yellow-500, Won grey-400) |
| Stat-bar palette (-400 family) | `#60a5fa #4ade80 #22d3ee #c084fc #f87171 #fbbf24` | Stat-card mini bars (accounts/activities/dashboard KPI strips), pinned by `tests/constants.test.ts` |
| Dialog/filter vocabularies (session-5) | Status: New/Contacted/Qualified/Unqualified · Sources: Call/Email/Website/Partner + 5 emoji contact sources · Event types: Meeting/Call/Demo/Task/Reminder/Appointment | DOM-extracted from the live dialogs, pinned by `tests/constants.test.ts` |

Placeholders ride `--color-muted-ink` (`#737373` — the session-10
measure), not `--color-subtle`. Contrast: all text pairs (`#0a0a0a`,
`#6b7280` on `#ffffff`/`#f9fafb`) exceed WCAG AA; the sidebar's
`#ffffff`-on-`#2563eb` exceeds AA at nav sizes. (Session-170: this table
was the LAST un-flipped color carrier — the s169 wave re-pinned SKILL
§4.1/§19 and left PAD §5.2 on the pre-session-7/12/13/14 hexes; every
row is now read live by `tests/doctrine-census.test.ts`.)

### 5.3 Component Primitives

Radix primitives (dialog, popover-based dropdown, select, label, slot) under
`src/components/ui/` with NEO CRM styling; tabs, tables, checkboxes, avatars
and toasts are hand-rolled with native ARIA (`role="tablist/tab/tabpanel"`,
`role="status"` toasts, native checkbox inputs). z-index scale: topbar `z-40`,
drawer/dialogs `z-50`, dropdown portals `z-[60]`, toasts `z-[100]`.

### 5.4 Motion / Animation

Dialog/dropdown enter-exit from the vendored `tw-animate-css`
(`data-[state=open]:animate-in`, `fade-in-0`, `zoom-in-95`); the mobile
drawer slides via a CSS `transition-transform` on a `translate-x` toggle with
a matched `transition-[visibility]` (so the closed state is truly hidden
after the animation completes). `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` collapses
all animations to 0.01ms via a global media rule.

---

## 6. Security Architecture

### 6.1 Security Rules

| # | Rule | Enforcement |
| - | ---- | ----------- |
| 1 | Every gated API handler requires a valid session | `requireSession()` first statement; call-form grep-verified across the 29 route files (19 gated; the 10 ungated: the public auth family's 7 verbs [rate-limited instead] + `me` [the data:null face, session-41] + `health` + the regex-pinned `uploads/[name]` serving) |
| 2 | Authenticated page group unreachable without a session | `(app)/layout.tsx` server redirect |
| 3 | Passwords stored as `scrypt:salt:hash` (64-byte derived key) | `hashPassword()` only; no plaintext paths exist |
| 4 | Session tokens HMAC-SHA256 signed, `timingSafeEqual` compared, 7-day TTL | `signSession` / `verifySessionToken` (unit-tested) |
| 5 | Cookies `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` in production, `Path=/` | `setSessionCookie` |
| 6 | Login + signup rate-limited 10 attempts / IP / 15 min | fixed-window limiter + `Retry-After` on 429 |
| 7 | All inputs trimmed, length-capped, enum-checked; IDs existence-checked | `parseBody` + `src/lib/schemas.ts` (the Zod seam) + `asString/asNumber/asDate/asFKId` on the URL-param family + per-handler referential checks |
| 8 | Destructive reset requires typing `RESET` + explicit confirm | `/api/reset` + Settings danger zone |
| 9 | No secrets in git (`.env`, `*.key`, `db/*.db` ignored; `.env` untracked) | `.gitignore` + SSH wrapper runbook (keys never inside the repo) |
| 10 | No `console.log` of secrets / tokens in shipped code | review + envelope error messages are user-safe |

### 6.2 Security Utilities

`src/lib/auth.ts` (scrypt, HMAC, cookie lifecycle), `src/lib/rate-limit.ts`
(fixed-window limiter with sweeper), `src/lib/api.ts` (guard + validation
coercers). The Prisma client parameterizes all queries (no raw SQL except
the health probe's `SELECT 1`).

### 6.3 Authentication & Authorization

Stateless cookie sessions (ADR-003). Roles: `admin` (first user) vs `rep` —
currently informational (owners/filters), no route-level RBAC yet (tracked
in Known Issues). The signup endpoint assigns `admin` to the first user only
(bootstrap); subsequent users are `rep`.

### 6.4 Threat Model

| Vector | Mitigation |
| ------ | ---------- |
| Credential stuffing | rate limiter + constant-time token compare + generic error copy |
| Session forgery/tampering | HMAC + `timingSafeEqual`; 64-hex signatures |
| XSS | React auto-escaping; no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` in app code; notes rendered as text |
| CSRF on mutations | `SameSite=Lax` cookies + JSON-only endpoints (cross-site form posts can't shape the body); no GET mutates anything except the explicit `?download=1` CSV export |
| SQLi | Prisma parameterization everywhere |
| Clickjacking | No embedded frames by design; standard `X-Frame-Options`/CSP left to the edge proxy (documented in Deployment) |
| Data loss | Danger-zone reset is double-gated (typed confirm + API confirm); backups are file-copies of `db/custom.db` (documented) |
